{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01MxniQHD6rQLJKZrP1SfyMg","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# AGENTS.md\n     2→\n     3→Guidance for AI coding agents working in this repository.\n     4→\n     5→## What this is\n     6→\n     7→A source-linked public graph of Washington State legislative activity — bills, hearings, testimony, video, transcripts, reviewed speakers, organizations, and public-record context — modeled on CalMatters Digital Democracy. Long-form ingestion walkthrough lives at **`docs/ingestion.md`** — read that before making changes to anything in `internal/jobs/` or `internal/sources/`.\n     8→\n     9→## Architecture in one minute\n    10→\n    11→```\n    12→nightly cron: make daily\n    13→  ├─ wa-dd ingest-legislators  LWS roster → person, legislator, memberships\n    14→  ├─ wa-dd ingest-session     LWS metadata for every bill in biennium +\n    15→  │                           CSI agenda IDs + TVW event IDs on hearing rows\n    16→  ├─ wa-dd ingest-irs-bmf-wa  IRS BMF Washington nonprofit reference data\n    17→  ├─ wa-dd ingest-pdc-employers\n    18→  │                           PDC lobbyist-employer registrations\n    19→  ├─ wa-dd ingest-hearings    full hearing pipeline (CSI testifiers +\n    20→  │                           Invintus event/media + diarization +\n    21→  │                           transcript segmentation + organization links)\n    22→  ├─ wa-dd verify-organizations\n    23→  │                           confirms existing orgs against IRS/PDC refs\n    24→  └─ wa-dd generate-vendor-entity-matches\n    25→                              creates reviewable source/org match candidates\n    26→\n    27→  → Postgres (single source of truth)\n    28→  → wa-dd-api on :8080 (chi router; reads only; Clerk-gated /admin routes)\n    29→  → Next.js on :3000 (Server Components fetch the API; ISR revalidate: 60;\n    30→                      /admin UI for speaker + org review)\n    31→```\n    32→\n    33→The `bill` / `legislator` / `bill_sponsor` / `bill_status_change` / `hearing` / `agenda_item` / `tvw_event` / `organization` tables are upsertable on stable keys — re-running passes is safe. `testifier` and `agenda_item_window` are replaced per agenda item; diarization rows are appended per provider job, but `ingest-hearings` checks for an existing succeeded diarization job and uses an event-level advisory lock to avoid duplicate provider submissions.\n    34→\n    35→## Layout\n    36→\n    37→```\n    38→cmd/\n    39→  wa-dd/        operator CLI. One file per subcommand (cmd_*.go). Covers\n    40→                ingestion (ingest-session, ingest-hearings,\n    41→                ingest-legislators, ingest-contracts, ingest-pdc-*,\n    42→                ingest-irs-bmf-wa, ingest-usaspending-wa-awards, …),\n    43→                diarization (audio-cache, diarize-event, diarize-pending,\n    44→                extract-speaker-evidence,\n    45→                backfill-speaker-evidence), and entity/org workflows\n    46→                (populate-organizations,\n    47→                generate-vendor-entity-matches, decide-entity-match,\n    48→                verify-organizations, prune-junk-organizations).\n    49→  wa-dd-api/    read-only HTTP API the Next.js frontend reads from (:8080).\n    50→                Routes split per resource (bill/hearing/org/...); registry in\n    51→                routes.go.\n    52→internal/\n    53→  sources/{lws,csi,committeeschedules,tvw,pdc,socrata,datawa,fiscalwa,\n    54→           usaspending,irsbmf,bls,census,epa,fema,hud,sao,seattle,\n    55→           seattleauditor,kingcounty,webs,connector,...}/\n    56→                connectors. Each follows Fetch / StoreRaw / Parse / Normalize.\n    57→                Phase status table lives in internal/sources/README.md.\n    58→  sources/httpx/ shared retry + per-host rate-limit client\n    59→  jobs/         pipeline step primitives (ingest-bill, ingest-csi, ingest-tvw,\n    60→                segment-transcript, populate-organizations, discover)\n    61→  diarization/  provider-neutral diarization (Provider interface, Deepgram +\n    62→                pyannoteAI implementations, MergeConsecutiveSegments,\n    63→                self-introduction evidence extractor).\n    64→  entitymatch/  organization/vendor entity-match candidate generation +\n    65→                decision recording.\n    66→  common/       shared civic value objects (BillKey, BillAgendaTarget)\n    67→  storage/db/   pgx wrapper, hand-written Pool.Query methods on *Store,\n    68→                BillAgendaTarget lookups and query helpers\n    69→db/migrations/  goose-style SQL; project-pinned via tools/goose\n    70→db/queries/     EMPTY. sqlc.yaml exists but the project uses hand-written\n    71→                Pool.Query methods on *Store, not codegen. Don't add to this\n    72→                directory unless explicitly migrating to sqlc.\n    73→apps/web/       Next.js 16 + React 19 + TS + Tailwind 4. Server Components\n    74→                fetch the Go API by absolute URL (process.env.WADD_API_URL)\n    75→                because they don't go through next.config.ts rewrites.\n    76→                Client components use the rewrite (/api/v1/* → :8080).\n    77→                /admin uses Clerk in production; bypassed in non-prod via\n    78→                NODE_ENV gate (see src/lib/adminAuth.ts and src/proxy.ts).\n    79→data/processed/ run-summary JSONs, diarization output, and derived\n    80→                artifacts (gitignored)\n    81→docs/           ingestion.md is the canonical implementation doc.\n    82→                hearing-diarization-and-review.md covers the diarization +\n    83→                speaker-review pipeline. organization-matching-and-review.md\n    84→                covers the org/vendor entity-match flow.\n    85→```\n    86→\n    87→## Common commands\n    88→\n    89→```sh\n    90→# Local dev environment\n    91→make up                     # start Postgres in Docker\n    92→make migrate-up             # apply migrations via project-pinned goose\n    93→make api                    # run wa-dd-api on :8080\n    94→cd apps/web && pnpm dev     # Next.js on :3000\n    95→\n    96→# Tests + gates — see \"Running tests\" below for full setup per category\n    97→go vet ./...\n    98→go test ./...                                       # Go unit tests, no infra\n    99→make integration                                    # Go integration tests (boots Postgres, seeds/cleans fixtures)\n   100→cd apps/web && pnpm typecheck                       # Frontend typecheck\n   101→make e2e                                            # Playwright e2e (seeds/cleans fixtures; requires Postgres + chromium)\n   102→\n   103→# Ingestion (operator-driven; usually triggered via make daily)\n   104→INVINTUS_EMBEDDER_KEY=… PYANNOTEAI_API_KEY=… make daily  # full nightly chain\n   105→go run ./cmd/wa-dd ingest-session --biennium 2025-26 --limit 25  # smoke\n   106→go run ./cmd/wa-dd ingest-hearings  --biennium 2025-26 --limit 5\n   107→\n   108→```\n   109→\n   110→`make help` lists every Makefile target with a one-line description.\n   111→\n   112→## Running tests\n   113→\n   114→Four categories. **Always use these exact commands** — don't improvise (e.g. `pnpm exec playwright` directly will fail without env vars).\n   115→\n   116→### 1. Go unit tests (no infra)\n   117→\n   118→```sh\n   119→go test ./...\n   120→go test ./internal/sources/lws/... -run TestParse   # single package + test\n   121→go vet ./...\n   122→```\n   123→\n   124→### 2. Go integration tests (need Postgres)\n   125→\n   126→```sh\n   127→make integration              # boots integration DB, seeds fixtures, runs go test -tags=integration ./..., cleans fixtures\n   128→# under the hood:\n   129→#   make integration-db       # docker compose up + migrate\n   130→#   scripts/seed-test-fixtures.sh\n   131→#   WADD_TEST_DSN=… go test -tags=integration ./...\n   132→#   scripts/cleanup-test-fixtures.sh\n   133→```\n   134→\n   135→For a single integration package: `WADD_TEST_DSN=\"postgres://wadd:wadd@localhost:5432/wa_dd?sslmode=disable\" go test -tags=integration ./cmd/wa-dd-api/...` (DB must already be up + seeded). If you seed fixtures manually, run `scripts/cleanup-test-fixtures.sh --dsn \"$WADD_TEST_DSN\"` afterward.\n   136→\n   137→### 3. Frontend typecheck / lint\n   138→\n   139→```sh\n   140→cd apps/web && pnpm install      # first time only\n   141→cd apps/web && pnpm typecheck\n   142→cd apps/web && pnpm lint\n   143→```\n   144→\n   145→### 4. End-to-end (Playwright + axe a11y)\n   146→\n   147→E2E spins up `wa-dd-api` and `next start` against a real Postgres, seeds deterministic fixtures for the run, cleans them afterward, then drives Chromium. **First-time setup is required** or every test will fail with \"browser not installed\" / \"module not found\".\n   148→\n   149→```sh\n   150→# One-time setup\n   151→make up                                 # Postgres in Docker\n   152→make migrate-up\n   153→cd apps/web && pnpm install             # installs @playwright/test, @axe-core/playwright, etc.\n   154→make e2e-install                        # downloads Chromium for Playwright\n   155→\n   156→# Run\n   157→make e2e                                # seeds fixtures, builds wa-dd-api + Next.js,\n   158→                                        # runs Playwright, then cleans fixtures\n   159→\n   160→# Single test (DB already seeded, binary already built)\n   161→cd apps/web && WADD_API_BIN=\"$(pwd)/../../bin/wa-dd-api\" \\\n   162→  WADD_E2E_DSN=\"postgres://wadd:wadd@localhost:5432/wa_dd?sslmode=disable\" \\\n   163→  pnpm exec playwright test -g \"accessibility landmarks\" --reporter=list\n   164→\n   165→# After manually seeded single-test runs\n   166→scripts/cleanup-test-fixtures.sh --dsn \"postgres://wadd:wadd@localhost:5432/wa_dd?sslmode=disable\"\n   167→```\n   168→\n   169→E2E tests live in `apps/web/e2e/`. Accessibility checks use `@axe-core/playwright` against WCAG 2.0/2.1 A+AA — a real violation fails the build, so fix the markup/styles rather than suppressing rules.\n   170→\n   171→## Conventions worth knowing\n   172→\n   173→- **Time zones.** WA legislative timestamps (LWS, TVW WP archive) are Pacific wall-clock without an explicit zone. Always parse with `time.ParseInLocation(..., \"America/Los_Angeles\")`. There's a fixed bug history here — see `internal/sources/lws/normalize.go:parseLWSDate` and `internal/jobs/discover.go:tvwPostsForDay`.\n   174→- **Bill prefixes.** LWS reports `BillID` in the *current* substituted/engrossed form (`SSB 6054`, `2SHB 1859`). `internal/sources/lws/normalize.go:baseBillPrefix` strips `E`/`N`/`S` chrome down to the bare prefix (`HB`/`SB`/`HJR`/etc.) so a single bill doesn't fork into multiple rows as it moves through the legislature.\n   175→- **`IngestBill` two-mode behavior.** When `Demo.Chamber` is set (curated path), it stores one chamber-matched hearing. When empty (`ingest-session` path), it stores every hearing LWS reports so the `ingest-session` discovery post-pass has rows to enrich.\n   176→- **Rate limit default 10 req/sec** per upstream host. The User-Agent identifies the project so state-agency operators can contact us. Retries on 429/5xx with exponential backoff.\n   177→- **Pipeline orchestration** lives in the CLI drivers. `Pipeline.RunMetadataOnly` runs only `IngestBill` for `ingest-session`; `ingest-hearings` iterates by `hearing_id`, runs CSI per agenda item, Invintus + diarization once per TVW event, transcript segmentation per agenda item, then hearing-scoped organization population.\n   178→- **Organizations.** CSI `raw_organization` strings seed `organization` rows as `possible`; `/organizations` shows only confirmed organizations. `verify-organizations` confirms unique IRS BMF/PDC employer normalized-name matches (IRS preferred over PDC). Confirmed entity-match decisions append the reviewed source name to `organization.aliases`, and organization upserts merge aliases instead of replacing them.\n   179→- **API response shapes.** Public API routes should return explicit response/list objects (`BillDetailResponse`, `HearingPage`, `OrganizationPage`, etc.). Keep collection fields initialized to `[]` rather than `nil` so frontend code can treat them as arrays.\n   180→- **No generated page snapshots.** Public/frontend page data comes from route-specific API objects assembled from Postgres.\n   181→- **API handler pattern.** `func handler(store *db.Store) http.HandlerFunc` returning a closure. Use the `writeJSON` envelope and `{\"error\": \"...\"}` for errors. Soft-parse query params (bad `limit=abc` falls back to default rather than 400) — see `billPageHandler` and `searchTranscriptsHandler` for examples.\n   182→- **Frontend fetch path.** Server Components fetch by absolute URL (`process.env.WADD_API_URL ?? \"http://localhost:8080\"`) because they don't traverse `next.config.ts` rewrites. Client components use the rewrite path `/api/v1/...` so requests stay same-origin.\n   183→- **Diarization providers.** `internal/diarization` is provider-neutral. pyannoteAI (`precision-2`) is the default for hearing ingestion and can return bundled transcription; Deepgram (`nova-3`) remains available as an alternate provider. See `docs/hearing-diarization-and-review.md` for tradeoffs and run commands.\n   184→- **Local admin auth.** `/admin` routes bypass Clerk when `NODE_ENV !== \"production\"` — see `apps/web/src/lib/adminAuth.ts`, `apps/web/src/proxy.ts`, and `cmd/wa-dd-api/auth.go`'s `localDevAdminBypass`. The web Dockerfile has a separate `web-dev` stage (`NODE_ENV=development`, `next dev`) used by `infra/docker-compose.yml`; the final `web` stage stays production-default for Railway.\n   185→- **Backwards compatibility** Typically, you do not need to make changes backwards compatible. Only include backwards compatibility if the user explicitly says so.\n   186→\n   187→## What lives where in the wiki\n   188→\n   189→- `~/Documents/v1/wiki/politics/Washington Digital Democracy - Comprehensive Plan.md` — current product/architecture record. The MVP phases through public beta are marked complete; do not reintroduce stale \"next steps\" or Phase 4 roadmap language unless Nolan asks.\n   190→- `~/Documents/v1/wiki/politics/Washington Digital Democracy - Canonical Data Sources.md` — source-of-truth list of upstream data sources.\n   191→- `~/Documents/v1/wiki/politics/data-sources/` — per-source connector specs.\n   192→- The wiki is the project-level intent; `docs/ingestion.md` is the implementation walkthrough.\n   193→\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it looks malicious. If it does, you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer high-level questions about the code behavior.\n</system-reminder>\n"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"eb99c7ea-79a9-43fe-8035-3155b41d721d","uuid":"d79d7b3b-b324-439b-b604-913c768f6487"}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01SLMcwcz7AyrFzsiUq1GWVg","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# Hearing diarization and speaker review\n     2→\n     3→Last updated: 2026-06-03.\n     4→\n     5→This document explains the WA Digital Democracy hearing diarization flow and the\n     6→manual human review that must happen before anonymous speaker clusters become\n     7→public speaker labels.\n     8→\n     9→The short version:\n    10→\n    11→1. `ingest-hearings` gets each hearing's CSI agenda/testifier data plus\n    12→   Invintus event, media, and audio/video metadata.\n    13→2. `ingest-hearings`, `diarize-event`, or `diarize-pending` sends the\n    14→   TVW/Invintus audio/video URL to a diarization provider. The normal hearing\n    15→   ingest path now runs diarization before transcript segmentation.\n    16→3. The provider output is normalized into anonymous speaker clusters and merged\n    17→   speech segments via `internal/diarization`'s provider-neutral types.\n    18→4. `extract-speaker-evidence` scans the diarized text for conservative identity\n    19→   clues, currently mostly self-introductions.\n    20→5. Human reviewers use `/admin/review/speakers` to accept, reject, request more\n    21→   evidence, or manually assign labels.\n    22→6. Public hearing transcripts render accepted `speaker_assignment` labels only;\n    23→   unresolved clusters remain anonymous/unreviewed.\n    24→\n    25→## Why this exists\n    26→\n    27→Diarization providers can answer **\"which spans sound like the same speaker?\"**\n    28→They cannot be trusted to answer **\"who is this person?\"** for public-facing\n    29→civic records.\n    30→\n    31→The system therefore separates:\n    32→\n    33→- diarization: anonymous time spans and cluster labels such as `SPEAKER_00`;\n    34→- evidence extraction: text/source clues that suggest a candidate identity;\n    35→- human review: a reviewer decides whether evidence is strong enough;\n    36→- public display: only accepted assignments become names on public transcript\n    37→  pages.\n    38→\n    39→This separation is intentional. It keeps the public product useful without\n    40→turning uncertain model output into authoritative identity claims.\n    41→\n    42→## Inputs and prerequisites\n    43→\n    44→A TVW event must already exist in Postgres. In the normal path this means:\n    45→\n    46→```sh\n    47→make ingest-legislators\n    48→make ingest-session BIENNIUM=2025-26\n    49→make ingest-hearings BIENNIUM=2025-26\n    50→```\n    51→\n    52→`ingest-session` discovers CSI agenda IDs and TVW event IDs. `ingest-hearings`\n    53→then fetches CSI testifiers, fetches Invintus event details/media assets, runs\n    54→diarization when the event has no successful diarization job yet, and segments\n    55→the diarized transcript into bill windows.\n    56→\n    57→Diarization requires:\n    58→\n    59→- a TVW/Invintus event ID (`tvw_event.tvw_event_id`);\n    60→- a usable audio/video source URL from `tvw_event` or `tvw_media_asset`;\n    61→- `PYANNOTEAI_API_KEY` for the default `ingest-hearings` provider, or\n    62→  `DEEPGRAM_API_KEY` / `--api-key` when explicitly using Deepgram;\n    63→- Postgres reachable through `WADD_DSN` or `--dsn`.\n    64→\n    65→## Data model\n    66→\n    67→The relevant tables are introduced mainly by:\n    68→\n    69→- `db/migrations/0012_diarization_audio.sql`\n    70→- `db/migrations/0013_entity_mentions.sql`\n    71→- `db/migrations/0014_speaker_review.sql`\n    72→\n    73→### Audio and diarization tables\n    74→\n    75→- `tvw_audio_asset`\n    76→  - Caches the selected source URL and optional normalized local audio metadata.\n    77→  - Tracks `source_url`, `source_kind`, local paths, content hash, duration,\n    78→    sample rate, channels, and codec.\n    79→\n    80→- `diarization_job`\n    81→  - One provider run for one TVW event.\n    82→  - Tracks provider, model, status (`pending`, `running`, `succeeded`, `failed`),\n    83→    raw result path, and errors.\n    84→  - The public/API path uses the latest succeeded job for a TVW event.\n    85→\n    86→- `speaker_cluster`\n    87→  - One anonymous speaker cluster from one diarization job.\n    88→  - Example labels: `SPEAKER_00`, `SPEAKER_01`.\n    89→  - Stores total speech time and turn count for prioritization/review.\n    90→\n    91→- `diarized_speech_segment`\n    92→  - Merged speech spans for the public transcript path.\n    93→  - Each row has `start_ms`, `end_ms`, text, confidence, and `cluster_label`.\n    94→  - Adjacent same-speaker Deepgram utterances are merged so the UI shows\n    95→    readable turns instead of sentence-level fragments.\n    96→\n    97→- `entity_mention`\n    98→  - Provider-derived named entities from Deepgram output.\n    99→  - These are evidence only; they are not confirmed organization/person facts.\n   100→\n   101→### Review tables\n   102→\n   103→- `speaker_identity_evidence`\n   104→  - Evidence row suggesting a candidate label for a speaker cluster.\n   105→  - Evidence types include `self_introduction`, `chair_call`, `entity_mention`,\n   106→    `csi_order`, and `manual_note`.\n   107→  - Current automatic extraction is intentionally conservative and mostly emits\n   108→    self-introduction evidence.\n   109→\n   110→- `speaker_review_task`\n   111→  - A proposed identity assignment that needs human action.\n   112→  - Status values: `pending`, `accepted`, `rejected`, `needs_more_evidence`,\n   113→    `superseded`.\n   114→\n   115→- `speaker_assignment`\n   116→  - The accepted public label for a cluster.\n   117→  - Unique per `(diarization_job_id, speaker_cluster_id)`.\n   118→  - Public hearing transcripts only show names through accepted assignments.\n   119→\n   120→## Provider normalization\n   121→\n   122→The provider boundary lives in `internal/diarization`.\n   123→\n   124→Two providers are supported: pyannoteAI (default for `ingest-hearings`) and\n   125→Deepgram. They share the `diarization.Provider` interface, so downstream\n   126→storage, evidence extraction, and the review UI are provider-agnostic.\n   127→\n   128→### Deepgram\n   129→\n   130→`diarize-event --provider deepgram` or\n   131→`ingest-hearings --provider deepgram` calls Deepgram's prerecorded endpoint\n   132→with:\n   133→\n   134→- `diarize=true`\n   135→- `punctuate=true`\n   136→- `utterances=true`\n   137→- `detect_entities=true`\n   138→- `model=<model>`, default `nova-3`\n   139→\n   140→Deepgram's diarizer is prone to **false-merge across long silences** —\n   141→unrelated speakers that share rough acoustic similarity sometimes collapse\n   142→into a single cluster after a multi-minute gap. We saw this on\n   143→`event_id=2026021094` where four distinct testifiers landed in `SPEAKER_58`.\n   144→That hearing motivated the pyannoteAI integration; reach for pyannoteAI\n   145→when speaker fidelity matters more than ASR quality.\n   146→\n   147→### pyannoteAI\n   148→\n   149→`diarize-event --provider pyannoteai` (model `precision-2`) submits an async\n   150→job to `https://api.pyannote.ai/v1/diarize` and polls `/v1/jobs/{id}` every\n   151→5s. Wall-clock is typically 15–30 min for a 1–2 hour hearing.\n   152→\n   153→By default the job is requested with `transcription=true` so the response\n   154→includes word- and turn-level transcripts already aligned to clusters. Each\n   155→turn becomes one `Segment` with `Text` populated, which feeds the same\n   156→evidence-extraction pipeline used for Deepgram. Set `--transcription=false`\n   157→for diarize-only. The current pyannoteAI submit API selects the ASR backend\n   158→server-side; the CLI does not send an ASR model field.\n   159→\n   160→Caveats:\n   161→\n   162→- Only `AudioInput.URL` is supported (no direct byte uploads). The TVW/\n   163→  Invintus source URL is publicly fetchable, so `--use-source-url=true`\n   164→  works as-is. MP4 video URLs are accepted even though pyannoteAI's docs\n   165→  only enumerate audio formats — they decode with ffmpeg server-side.\n   166→- Result JSON expires 24 hours after job completion; `diarize-event`\n   167→  persists `Raw` to `data/processed/diarization/...` so re-merge stays\n   168→  possible.\n   169→- pyannoteAI requires a work-domain email at signup (no Gmail/Outlook).\n   170→\n   171→The normalized provider-neutral result contains:\n   172→\n   173→- `Segments`: start/end milliseconds, anonymous speaker cluster, confidence,\n   174→  text;\n   175→- `Words`: optional word-level timing and speaker info;\n   176→- `Entities`: provider-detected named entities;\n   177→- `Raw`: raw provider JSON, written to `data/processed/diarization/...`.\n   178→\n   179→`internal/diarization.MergeConsecutiveSegments` collapses adjacent segments from\n   180→the same speaker cluster into one turn-like segment. This is the canonical\n   181→public transcript unit today.\n   182→\n   183→## Running diarization\n   184→\n   185→### One event\n   186→\n   187→For a single TVW/Invintus event ID:\n   188→\n   189→```sh\n   190→PYANNOTEAI_API_KEY=... \\\n   191→  go run ./cmd/wa-dd diarize-event \\\n   192→    --event-id <tvw_event_id>\n   193→```\n   194→\n   195→Useful flags:\n   196→\n   197→```txt\n   198→--provider pyannoteai            # or deepgram\n   199→--model precision-2              # pyannoteAI default; deepgram defaults to nova-3\n   200→--out-dir data/processed/diarization\n   201→--use-source-url=true            # default: send provider the TVW/Invintus URL\n   202→--api-key ...                    # defaults to DEEPGRAM_API_KEY or PYANNOTEAI_API_KEY\n   203→--transcription=true             # pyannoteai only: bundle ASR with diarization\n   204→--dsn ...                        # defaults to WADD_DSN or local dev DSN\n   205→```\n   206→\n   207→By default the command sends the original TVW/Invintus URL to the provider. If\n   208→a provider cannot fetch the source URL, run `audio-cache` first and then call\n   209→`diarize-event --use-source-url=false`.\n   210→\n   211→### Audio cache fallback\n   212→\n   213→```sh\n   214→go run ./cmd/wa-dd audio-cache --event-id <tvw_event_id>\n   215→PYANNOTEAI_API_KEY=... \\\n   216→  go run ./cmd/wa-dd diarize-event \\\n   217→    --event-id <tvw_event_id> \\\n   218→    --use-source-url=false\n   219→```\n   220→\n   221→`audio-cache` downloads and normalizes a TVW/Invintus audio/video source and\n   222→stores metadata in `tvw_audio_asset`. The local normalized path is then used for\n   223→upload mode.\n   224→\n   225→### Pending events\n   226→\n   227→To process events that have audio/video source URLs and no successful\n   228→diarization job yet:\n   229→\n   230→```sh\n   231→PYANNOTEAI_API_KEY=... \\\n   232→  go run ./cmd/wa-dd diarize-pending --limit 25 --concurrency 4\n   233→```\n   234→\n   235→Useful flags:\n   236→\n   237→```txt\n   238→--dry-run             # print pending event IDs without calling provider\n   239→--limit N             # cap event count\n   240→--concurrency N       # default 10; lower this if provider/API limits bite\n   241→--max-attempts N      # default 5 per event\n   242→```\n   243→\n   244→`diarize-pending` retries transient failures. Each event gets its own\n   245→`diarization_job`; failed jobs preserve the error for debugging.\n   246→\n   247→## Extracting speaker evidence\n   248→\n   249→Diarization creates anonymous clusters. The next step is to generate reviewable\n   250→identity candidates:\n   251→\n   252→```sh\n   253→go run ./cmd/wa-dd extract-speaker-evidence \\\n   254→  --event-id <tvw_event_id> \\\n   255→  --job-id <diarization_job_id>\n   256→```\n   257→\n   258→Optional:\n   259→\n   260→```txt\n   261→--limit N     # scan at most N diarized segments\n   262→```\n   263→\n   264→Current automatic extraction lives in `internal/diarization/speaker_evidence.go`.\n   265→It intentionally emits few candidates because false positives are worse than\n   266→misses. Current high-precision patterns include:\n   267→\n   268→- `for the record, Senator Jane Smith ...`\n   269→- `Representative Jane Smith from the ... district ...`\n   270→- `for the record, my name is Jane Smith ...`\n   271→- `my name is Jane Smith ...`\n   272→\n   273→For each candidate, the command:\n   274→\n   275→1. resolves the candidate against known legislator/testifier context when\n   276→   possible;\n   277→2. writes or updates `speaker_identity_evidence`;\n   278→3. writes or updates a `speaker_review_task` for the cluster/candidate;\n   279→4. assigns higher priority to legislator candidates and candidates with a\n   280→   resolved source ID.\n   281→\n   282→Evidence extraction does **not** publish a name. It only creates tasks.\n   283→\n   284→## Manual human review\n   285→\n   286→Review is required before names appear publicly.\n   287→\n   288→### Review entry points\n   289→\n   290→- `/admin/review/speakers`\n   291→  - event-level overview of diarized events needing review;\n   292→  - shows cluster counts, accepted assignments, pending task counts, unresolved\n   293→    clusters, and total speech time.\n   294→\n   295→- `/admin/review/speakers/events/<tvwEventId>`\n   296→  - lists clusters for one TVW event;\n   297→  - links into the cluster review page.\n   298→\n   299→- `/admin/review/speakers/clusters/<clusterId>`\n   300→  - main review screen for a speaker cluster;\n   301→  - shows candidate tasks, current accepted assignment, evidence rows, and\n   302→    sample segments;\n   303→  - supports accepting/rejecting candidate tasks, marking “more evidence,” and\n   304→    manual assignment.\n   305→\n   306→- `/admin/review/speakers/<taskId>`\n   307→  - older task-centered detail route.\n   308→\n   309→### What reviewers should check\n   310→\n   311→For each cluster, review:\n   312→\n   313→1. **Evidence text**\n   314→   - Does the transcript text actually identify the speaker?\n   315→   - Is the name complete enough for a public label?\n   316→   - Could the text be quoting someone else rather than self-identifying?\n   317→\n   318→2. **Timing and surrounding context**\n   319→   - Open the TVW timestamp if needed.\n   320→   - Check whether the same cluster speaks consistently before/after the\n   321→     evidence segment.\n   322→   - Watch for chair/staff transitions where diarization may accidentally merge\n   323→     speakers.\n   324→\n   325→3. **Candidate kind**\n   326→   - `legislator`: should match a known LWS sponsor/legislator when possible.\n   327→   - `testifier`: should be consistent with CSI testifier list/order and the\n   328→     bill/agenda context.\n   329→   - `person`: acceptable for manual/freeform labels when no structured ID is\n   330→     available.\n   331→   - `unknown`: use when no public label should be assigned.\n   332→\n   333→4. **Cluster quality**\n   334→   - A cluster may contain more than one real person if diarization merged\n   335→     voices incorrectly.\n   336→   - A real person may appear in multiple clusters if diarization split them.\n   337→   - If uncertain, reject or mark “more evidence” rather than accepting.\n   338→\n   339→5. **Public-risk posture**\n   340→   - Names on public pages should be reviewed, source-supported, and not\n   341→     speculative.\n   342→   - If the evidence is weak, leave the cluster anonymous.\n   343→\n   344→### Review actions\n   345→\n   346→- **Accept**\n   347→  - Marks the review task `accepted`.\n   348→  - Upserts `speaker_assignment` for the cluster.\n   349→  - The accepted label can then appear in public hearing transcripts.\n   350→\n   351→- **Reject**\n   352→  - Marks the candidate task `rejected`.\n   353→  - Does not create or change a public assignment.\n   354→\n   355→- **More evidence**\n   356→  - Marks the task `needs_more_evidence`.\n   357→  - Use when the candidate might be right but the current evidence is\n   358→    insufficient.\n   359→\n   360→- **Manual assignment**\n   361→  - Directly assigns a label to a cluster when the reviewer has enough evidence\n   362→    not captured by automatic extraction.\n   363→  - Requires choosing a kind (`person`, `legislator`, `testifier`, `unknown`),\n   364→    entering a label, reviewer, and optional notes.\n   365→\n   366→## Public display rules\n   367→\n   368→Public hearing pages call `ListDiarizedSegmentsByTVWEvent` through the hearing\n   369→API. That query joins the latest succeeded `diarization_job` for the TVW event\n   370→against accepted `speaker_assignment` rows.\n   371→\n   372→Rules:\n   373→\n   374→- reviewed/accepted assignment → show `speaker_label`;\n   375→- no accepted assignment → do **not** publish a name;\n   376→- unresolved cluster with `cluster_label` → show an unreviewed/anonymous speaker\n   377→  affordance in the UI;\n   378→- rejected or needs-more-evidence task → not public as a label;\n   379→- provider entity mentions → evidence only, not public identity facts.\n   380→\n   381→The frontend helper `PublicSpeakerLabel()` intentionally returns an empty string\n   382→unless the segment is reviewed. This is the safety gate that keeps unresolved\n   383→clusters from becoming authoritative facts.\n   384→\n   385→## Quality-control checklist\n   386→\n   387→Before treating a hearing as reviewed enough for public use:\n   388→\n   389→- [ ] The event has a succeeded `diarization_job`.\n   390→- [ ] Segments are merged into readable same-speaker turns.\n   391→- [ ] `extract-speaker-evidence` has been run for the job.\n   392→- [ ] High-priority pending speaker tasks have been accepted, rejected, or\n   393→      marked needs-more-evidence.\n   394→- [ ] Accepted labels are supported by transcript/source/video context.\n   395→- [ ] Ambiguous clusters remain anonymous.\n   396→- [ ] Public page rendering shows reviewed labels only.\n   397→- [ ] Source links/timestamps let a reader verify the underlying moment.\n   398→\n   399→## Common failure modes\n   400→\n   401→### No audio source found\n   402→\n   403→`diarize-event` depends on `BestTVWAudioSource` / `EnsureTVWAudioAsset`. If no\n   404→usable URL exists, re-run TVW ingest for the hearing and inspect `tvw_event` and\n   405→`tvw_media_asset` rows.\n   406→\n   407→### Provider cannot fetch URL\n   408→\n   409→Run `audio-cache --event-id ...` and retry `diarize-event --use-source-url=false`.\n   410→This uploads local normalized WAV bytes instead of asking Deepgram to fetch the\n   411→remote URL.\n   412→\n   413→### Bad speaker candidate\n   414→\n   415→Reject the task. If the text pattern itself is too permissive, adjust\n   416→`internal/diarization/speaker_evidence.go` and add a unit test.\n   417→\n   418→### Missing obvious speaker candidate\n   419→\n   420→Use manual assignment if the evidence is clear. Then consider adding a new\n   421→high-precision extractor only if it is likely to generalize without creating\n   422→false positives.\n   423→\n   424→### Wrong accepted assignment\n   425→\n   426→Use the cluster review page to assign the correct label manually. The\n   427→`speaker_assignment` table is unique per cluster/job, so a new accepted\n   428→assignment supersedes the old public label.\n   429→\n   430→## Useful SQL snippets\n   431→\n   432→Latest successful job for an event:\n   433→\n   434→```sql\n   435→SELECT id, tvw_event_id, provider, model, status, finished_at, raw_result_path\n   436→  FROM diarization_job\n   437→ WHERE tvw_event_id = '<event_id>' AND status = 'succeeded'\n   438→ ORDER BY finished_at DESC NULLS LAST, id DESC\n   439→ LIMIT 1;\n   440→```\n   441→\n   442→Clusters needing review:\n   443→\n   444→```sql\n   445→SELECT sc.id, sc.cluster_label, sc.total_speech_ms, sc.turn_count,\n   446→       COUNT(t.id) FILTER (WHERE t.status = 'pending') AS pending_tasks,\n   447→       COALESCE(sa.speaker_label, '') AS accepted_label\n   448→  FROM speaker_cluster sc\n   449→  JOIN diarization_job j ON j.id = sc.diarization_job_id\n   450→  LEFT JOIN speaker_review_task t ON t.speaker_cluster_id = sc.id\n   451→  LEFT JOIN speaker_assignment sa\n   452→    ON sa.speaker_cluster_id = sc.id\n   453→   AND sa.review_status = 'accepted'\n   454→ WHERE sc.tvw_event_id = '<event_id>'\n   455→   AND j.status = 'succeeded'\n   456→ GROUP BY sc.id, sc.cluster_label, sc.total_speech_ms, sc.turn_count, sa.speaker_label\n   457→ ORDER BY pending_tasks DESC, sc.total_speech_ms DESC;\n   458→```\n   459→\n   460→Accepted assignments for an event:\n   461→\n   462→```sql\n   463→SELECT sc.cluster_label, sa.speaker_kind, sa.speaker_label, sa.review_status,\n   464→       sa.updated_at\n   465→  FROM speaker_assignment sa\n   466→  JOIN speaker_cluster sc ON sc.id = sa.speaker_cluster_id\n   467→ WHERE sc.tvw_event_id = '<event_id>'\n   468→   AND sa.review_status = 'accepted'\n   469→ ORDER BY sc.cluster_label;\n   470→```\n   471→\n   472→## Safety principles\n   473→\n   474→- Diarization clusters are anonymous until reviewed.\n   475→- Model-detected entities are evidence, not confirmed facts.\n   476→- Prefer false negatives over false positives for public speaker labels.\n   477→- Keep raw provider JSON and source records so decisions can be audited.\n   478→- Humans, not models, decide public identity labels.\n   479→- When in doubt, leave the speaker anonymous and preserve the timestamped\n   480→  evidence trail.\n   481→\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you raw_organization matches any of the supplied raw names (case-insensitive).\n    87→// Used by Step 7 (org context) to bind testifiers to their canonical org.\n    88→func (s *Store) LinkTestifiersToOrg(ctx context.Context, orgID int64, rawOrgNames []string) (int64, error) {\n    89→\tif len(rawOrgNames) == 0 {\n    90→\t\treturn 0, nil\n    91→\t}\n    92→\tlowers := make([]string, len(rawOrgNames))\n    93→\tfor i, n := range rawOrgNames {\n    94→\t\tlowers[i] = lowerTrim(n)\n    95→\t}\n    96→\tconst q = `\n    97→UPDATE testifier SET normalized_org_id = $1\n    98→ WHERE lower(trim(raw_organization)) = ANY($2);`\n    99→\ttag, err := s.Pool.Exec(ctx, q, orgID, lowers)\n   100→\tif err != nil {\n   101→\t\treturn 0, err\n   102→\t}\n   103→\tif tag.RowsAffected() == 0 {\n   104→\t\treturn 0, nil\n   105→\t}\n   106→\tif err := s.BackfillCSITestifierPersonAffiliationsForRawOrganizations(ctx, rawOrgNames); err != nil {\n   107→\t\treturn 0, err\n   108→\t}\n   109→\treturn tag.RowsAffected(), nil\n   110→}\n   111→\n   112→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   113→// bill_status_change\n   114→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   115→\n   116→// OrganizationAggregate is the row shape ListOrganizations returns.\n   117→type OrganizationAggregate struct {\n   118→\tID              int64\n   119→\tCanonicalName   string\n   120→\tAliases         []string\n   121→\tMatchConfidence string\n   122→\tMatchNotes      string\n   123→\tTestifierCount  int\n   124→\tProCount        int\n   125→\tConCount        int\n   126→\tOtherCount      int\n   127→\tUnknownCount    int\n   128→}\n   129→\n   130→// ListOrganizations returns every organization with aggregated testifier\n   131→// position counts. When topicKeywords is non-empty, aggregation is scoped to\n   132→// testifiers whose agenda item is on a bill matching any keyword (matched\n   133→// against bill number/title/description, agenda item label, or committee\n   134→// name) and organizations with no qualifying testifiers are dropped.\n   135→func (s *Store) ListOrganizations(ctx context.Context, topicKeywords []string) ([]OrganizationAggregate, error) {\n   136→\tkeywords := nonEmptyStrings(topicKeywords)\n   137→\targs := []any{}\n   138→\tpush := func(v any) int { args = append(args, v); return len(args) }\n   139→\n   140→\ttestifierWhere := \"\"\n   141→\tif len(keywords) > 0 {\n   142→\t\tors := make([]string, 0, len(keywords))\n   143→\t\tfor _, kw := range keywords {\n   144→\t\t\tidx := push(kw)\n   145→\t\t\tors = append(ors, fmt.Sprintf(`(\n   146→\t\tb.bill_number ILIKE '%%' || $%d || '%%'\n   147→\t\tOR b.title ILIKE '%%' || $%d || '%%'\n   148→\t\tOR COALESCE(b.description, '') ILIKE '%%' || $%d || '%%'\n   149→\t\tOR a.label ILIKE '%%' || $%d || '%%'\n   150→\t\tOR h.committee_name ILIKE '%%' || $%d || '%%'\n   151→)`, idx, idx, idx, idx, idx))\n   152→\t\t}\n   153→\t\ttestifierWhere = `\n   154→       AND EXISTS (\n   155→         SELECT 1\n   156→           FROM agenda_item a\n   157→           JOIN hearing  h ON h.id = a.hearing_id\n   158→           LEFT JOIN bill b ON b.id = a.bill_id\n   159→          WHERE a.id = t.agenda_item_id\n   160→            AND (` + strings.Join(ors, \" OR \") + `)\n   161→       )`\n   162→\t}\n   163→\n   164→\tq := `\n   165→SELECT o.id, o.canonical_name, o.aliases,\n   166→       o.match_confidence::text, COALESCE(o.match_notes, ''),\n   167→       COUNT(DISTINCT t.id) AS testifier_count,\n   168→       COUNT(DISTINCT t.id) FILTER (WHERE t.position = 'Pro')   AS pro_count,\n   169→       COUNT(DISTINCT t.id) FILTER (WHERE t.position = 'Con')   AS con_count,\n   170→       COUNT(DISTINCT t.id) FILTER (WHERE t.position = 'Other') AS other_count,\n   171→       COUNT(DISTINCT t.id) FILTER (WHERE t.position = 'Unknown') AS unknown_count\n   172→  FROM organization o\n   173→  LEFT JOIN testifier t ON t.normalized_org_id = o.id` + testifierWhere + `\n   174→ GROUP BY o.id`\n   175→\tif len(keywords) > 0 {\n   176→\t\tq += \"\\nHAVING COUNT(DISTINCT t.id) > 0\"\n   177→\t}\n   178→\tq += \"\\n ORDER BY o.canonical_name;\"\n   179→\n   180→\trows, err := s.Pool.Query(ctx, q, args...)\n   181→\tif err != nil {\n   182→\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"list organizations: %w\", err)\n   183→\t}\n   184→\tdefer rows.Close()\n   185→\tout := []OrganizationAggregate{}\n   186→\tfor rows.Next() {\n   187→\t\tvar o OrganizationAggregate\n   188→\t\tif err := rows.Scan(&o.ID, &o.CanonicalName, &o.Aliases,\n   189→\t\t\t&o.MatchConfidence, &o.MatchNotes,\n   190→\t\t\t&o.TestifierCount, &o.ProCount, &o.ConCount,\n   191→\t\t\t&o.OtherCount, &o.UnknownCount); err != nil {\n   192→\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"scan organization: %w\", err)\n   193→\t\t}\n   194→\t\tout = append(out, o)\n   195→\t}\n   196→\treturn out, rows.Err()\n   197→}\n   198→\n   199→// OrganizationAppearance is one (org, agenda_item) row.\n   200→type OrganizationAppearance struct {\n   201→\tBiennium        string\n   202→\tBillID          string\n   203→\tBillPrefix      string\n   204→\tBillNumber      int\n   205→\tCSIAgendaItemID string\n   206→\tHearingID       int64\n   207→\tHearingTitle    string\n   208→\tCommitteeName   string\n   209→\tChamber         string\n   210→\tMeetingDateTime time.Time\n   211→\tPosition        string\n   212→\tTestifierCount  int\n   213→}\n   214→\n   215→type OrganizationPublicContext struct {\n   216→\tContextType     string\n   217→\tSourceKind      string\n   218→\tSourceLabel     string\n   219→\tSourceName      string\n   220→\tDetail          string\n   221→\tAmount          string\n   222→\tRecordYear      int\n   223→\tRecordDate      string\n   224→\tURL             string\n   225→\tMatchConfidence string\n   226→\tEvidence        []string\n   227→}\n   228→\n   229→type OrganizationPersonAffiliation struct {\n   230→\tPersonID            int64\n   231→\tPersonName          string\n   232→\tRelationshipType    string\n   233→\tRoleTitle           string\n   234→\tSourceKind          string\n   235→\tSourceLabel         string\n   236→\tRawOrganizationName string\n   237→\tRecordYears         string\n   238→\tSourceCount         int\n   239→\tConfidence          string\n   240→}\n   241→\n   242→// GetOrganizationAppearances returns every (agenda_item, org) appearance\n   243→// where at least one testifier from that org signed in.\n   244→func (s *Store) GetOrganizationAppearances(ctx context.Context, organizationID int64) ([]OrganizationAppearance, error) {\n   245→\tconst q = `\n   246→SELECT b.biennium, b.bill_number, b.prefix, b.number,\n   247→       COALESCE(a.csi_agenda_item_id, ''),\n   248→       h.id,\n   249→       COALESCE(a.label, ''),\n   250→       h.committee_name,\n   251→       COALESCE(h.chamber, ''),\n   252→       h.meeting_datetime,\n   253→       MAX(t.position::text) AS position,\n   254→       COUNT(t.id) AS testifier_count\n   255→  FROM testifier t\n   256→  JOIN agenda_item a ON a.id = t.agenda_item_id\n   257→  JOIN hearing     h ON h.id = a.hearing_id\n   258→  LEFT JOIN bill   b ON b.id = a.bill_id\n   259→ WHERE t.normalized_org_id = $1\n   260→ GROUP BY b.biennium, b.bill_number, b.prefix, b.number,\n   261→          a.csi_agenda_item_id, h.id, a.label, h.committee_name, h.chamber, h.meeting_datetime\n   262→ ORDER BY h.meeting_datetime DESC;`\n   263→\trows, err := s.Pool.Query(ctx, q, organizationID)\n   264→\tif err != nil {\n   265→\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"organization appearances: %w\", err)\n   266→\t}\n   267→\tdefer rows.Close()\n   268→\tout := []OrganizationAppearance{}\n   269→\tfor rows.Next() {\n   270→\t\tvar a OrganizationAppearance\n   271→\t\tvar biennium, billID, prefix *string\n   272→\t\tvar billNumber *int\n   273→\t\tif err := rows.Scan(&biennium, &billID, &prefix, &billNumber,\n   274→\t\t\t&a.CSIAgendaItemID, &a.HearingID, &a.HearingTitle,\n   275→\t\t\t&a.CommitteeName, &a.Chamber, &a.MeetingDateTime,\n   276→\t\t\t&a.Position, &a.TestifierCount); err != nil {\n   277→\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"scan appearance: %w\", err)\n   278→\t\t}\n   279→\t\tif biennium != nil {\n   280→\t\t\ta.Biennium = *biennium\n   281→\t\t}\n   282→\t\tif billID != nil {\n   283→\t\t\ta.BillID = *billID\n   284→\t\t}\n   285→\t\tif prefix != nil {\n   286→\t\t\ta.BillPrefix = *prefix\n   287→\t\t}\n   288→\t\tif billNumber != nil {\n   289→\t\t\ta.BillNumber = *billNumber\n   290→\t\t}\n   291→\t\tout = append(out, a)\n   292→\t}\n   293→\treturn out, rows.Err()\n   294→}\n   295→\n   296→func (s *Store) GetOrganizationPersonAffiliations(ctx context.Context, organizationID int64) ([]OrganizationPersonAffiliation, error) {\n   297→\tconst q = `\n   298→SELECT COALESCE(p.id, 0) AS person_id,\n   299→       COALESCE(NULLIF(p.display_name, ''), NULLIF(poa.raw_person_name, ''), 'Unknown person') AS person_name,\n   300→       poa.relationship_type::text,\n   301→       COALESCE(NULLIF(poa.role_title, ''), '') AS role_title,\n   302→       poa.source_kind::text,\n   303→       CASE poa.source_kind::text\n   304→         WHEN 'csi_testifier' THEN 'Committee Sign In'\n   305→         WHEN 'pdc_lobbyist_employment' THEN 'PDC lobbyist employment'\n   306→         WHEN 'pdc_lobbyist_compensation' THEN 'PDC lobbyist compensation'\n   307→         WHEN 'pdc_contribution' THEN 'PDC contribution'\n   308→         WHEN 'webs_vendor_contact' THEN 'WEBS vendor contact'\n   309→         WHEN 'deepgram_speaker' THEN 'Transcript speaker'\n   310→         WHEN 'legislator_roster' THEN 'Legislator roster'\n   311→         ELSE poa.source_kind::text\n   312→       END AS source_label,\n   313→       COALESCE(NULLIF(poa.raw_organization_name, ''), '') AS raw_organization_name,\n   314→       COALESCE(\n   315→         array_to_string(\n   316→           array_agg(DISTINCT poa.record_year ORDER BY poa.record_year DESC)\n   317→             FILTER (WHERE poa.record_year IS NOT NULL),\n   318→           ', '\n   319→         ),\n   320→         ''\n   321→       ) AS record_years,\n   322→       COUNT(*)::int AS source_count,\n   323→       CASE\n   324→         WHEN bool_or(poa.confidence = 'confirmed') THEN 'confirmed'\n   325→         WHEN bool_or(poa.confidence = 'probable') THEN 'probable'\n   326→         WHEN bool_or(poa.confidence = 'possible') THEN 'possible'\n   327→         ELSE 'unmatched'\n   328→       END AS confidence\n   329→  FROM person_organization_affiliation poa\n   330→  LEFT JOIN person p ON p.id = poa.person_id\n   331→ WHERE poa.organization_id = $1\n   332→ GROUP BY p.id, p.display_name, poa.raw_person_name, poa.relationship_type,\n   333→          poa.role_title, poa.source_kind, poa.raw_organization_name\n   334→ ORDER BY CASE poa.relationship_type::text\n   335→            WHEN 'lobbyist_for' THEN 0\n   336→            WHEN 'testified_for' THEN 1\n   337→            WHEN 'signed_in_for' THEN 2\n   338→            ELSE 3\n   339→          END,\n   340→          person_name;`\n   341→\trows, err := s.Pool.Query(ctx, q, organizationID)\n   342→\tif err != nil {\n   343→\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"organization person affiliations: %w\", err)\n   344→\t}\n   345→\tdefer rows.Close()\n   346→\tout := []OrganizationPersonAffiliation{}\n   347→\tfor rows.Next() {\n   348→\t\tvar a OrganizationPersonAffiliation\n   349→\t\tif err := rows.Scan(&a.PersonID, &a.PersonName, &a.RelationshipType, &a.RoleTitle,\n   350→\t\t\t&a.SourceKind, &a.SourceLabel, &a.RawOrganizationName, &a.RecordYears,\n   351→\t\t\t&a.SourceCount, &a.Confidence); err != nil {\n   352→\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"scan organization person affiliation: %w\", err)\n   353→\t\t}\n   354→\t\tout = append(out, a)\n   355→\t}\n   356→\treturn out, rows.Err()\n   357→}\n   358→\n   359→func (s *Store) GetOrganizationPublicContexts(ctx context.Context, organizationID int64) ([]OrganizationPublicContext, error) {\n   360→\tconst q = `\n   361→WITH ctx AS (\n   362→  SELECT 'lobbying_registration'::text AS context_type,\n   363→         m.source_kind::text AS source_kind,\n   364→         'PDC lobbying employer'::text AS source_label,\n   365→         pe.name AS source_name,\n   366→         NULLIF('Employer ID ' || pe.employer_id, '') AS detail,\n   367→         ''::text AS amount,\n   368→         CASE WHEN pe.last_employment_year ~ '^[0-9]+$' THEN pe.last_employment_year::int ELSE 0 END AS record_year,\n   369→         ''::text AS record_date,\n   370→         COALESCE(pe.last_employment_url, '') AS url,\n   371→           m.match_confidence::text AS match_confidence,\n   372→         m.evidence AS evidence\n   373→    FROM (\n   374→      SELECT DISTINCT ON (source_row_id) *\n   375→        FROM reviewed_vendor_entity_match\n   376→       WHERE organization_id = $1\n   377→         AND source_kind = 'pdc_lobbying_organization'\n   378→       ORDER BY source_row_id, reviewed_at DESC\n   379→    ) m\n   380→    JOIN LATERAL (\n   381→      SELECT *\n   382→        FROM pdc_employer pe2\n   383→       WHERE pe2.employer_id = m.source_row_id\n   384→       ORDER BY pe2.last_employment_year DESC NULLS LAST, pe2.id DESC\n   385→       LIMIT 1\n   386→    ) pe ON TRUE\n   387→  UNION ALL\n   388→  SELECT 'state_contract',\n   389→         m.source_kind::text,\n   390→         'DataWA agency contract',\n   391→         c.contractor_name,\n   392→         COALESCE(NULLIF(c.contract_number, ''), NULLIF(c.description, ''), 'Agency contract'),\n   393→         COALESCE(c.total_amount::text, ''),\n   394→         COALESCE(c.fiscal_year, 0),\n   395→         '',\n   396→         '',\n   397→         0,\n   398→         m.match_confidence::text,\n   399→         m.evidence\n   400→    FROM reviewed_vendor_entity_match m\n   401→    JOIN datawa_contract c ON c.id = m.source_pk\n   402→   WHERE m.organization_id = $1\n   403→     AND m.source_kind = 'datawa_contract_contractor'\n   404→  UNION ALL\n   405→  SELECT 'state_contract',\n   406→         m.source_kind::text,\n   407→         'DataWA master contract sale',\n   408→         s.vendor_name,\n   409→         COALESCE(NULLIF(s.contract_number, ''), NULLIF(s.contract_title, ''), 'Master contract sale'),\n   410→         COALESCE(s.total_sales_reported::text, ''),\n   411→         COALESCE(s.report_year, 0),\n   412→         '',\n   413→         '',\n   414→         0,\n   415→         m.match_confidence::text,\n   416→         m.evidence\n   417→    FROM 'datawa_master_contract_vendor'\n   421→  UNION ALL\n   422→  SELECT 'state_contract',\n   423→         m.source_kind::text,\n   424→         'DataWA IT contract',\n   425→         c.contractor_name,\n   426→         COALESCE(NULLIF(c.contract_number, ''), 'IT contract'),\n   427→         COALESCE(c.total_contract_amount::text, ''),\n   428→         COALESCE(c.report_fiscal_year, 0),\n   429→         '',\n   430→         '',\n   431→         0,\n   432→         m.match_confidence::text,\n   433→         m.evidence\n   434→    FROM reviewed_vendor_entity_match m\n   435→    JOIN datawa_it_contract c ON c.id = m.source_pk\n   436→   WHERE m.organization_id = $1\n   437→     AND m.source_kind IN ('datawa_it_contract_contractor', 'datawa_it_contract_dba')\n   438→  UNION ALL\n   439→  SELECT 'state_vendor',\n   440→         m.source_kind::text,\n   441→         'WEBS vendor',\n   442→         v.company_name,\n   443→         COALESCE(NULLIF(v.description_of_work, ''), NULLIF(v.commodity_code, ''), 'WEBS vendor registration'),\n   444→         '',\n   445→         0,\n   446→         '',\n   447→         COALESCE(v.web_address, ''),\n   448→         0,\n   449→         m.match_confidence::text,\n   450→         m.evidence\n   451→    FROM reviewed_vendor_entity_match m\n   452→    JOIN  457→         m.source_kind::text,\n   458→         'FiscalWA vendor payment',\n   459→         p.vendor_name,\n   460→         COALESCE(NULLIF(p.agency_name, ''), NULLIF(p.subobject_name, ''), 'Vendor payment'),\n   461→         COALESCE(p.amount::text, ''),\n   462→         COALESCE(p.fiscal_year, 0),\n   463→         '',\n   464→         '',\n   465→         0,\n   466→         m.match_confidence::text,\n   467→         m.evidence\n   468→    FROM reviewed_vendor_entity_match m\n   469→    JOIN fiscalwa_vendor_payment p ON p.id = m.source_pk\n   470→   WHERE m.organization_id = $1\n   471→     AND m.source_kind = 'fiscalwa_vendor_payment'\n   472→  UNION ALL\n   473→  SELECT 'federal_award',\n   474→         m.source_kind::text,\n   475→         'USAspending award',\n   476→         a.recipient_name,\n   477→         COALESCE(NULLIF(a.awarding_agency, ''), NULLIF(a.funding_agency, ''), a.award_id),\n   478→         COALESCE(a.award_amount::text, ''),\n   479→         COALESCE(EXTRACT(YEAR FROM a.start_date)::int, 0),\n   480→         COALESCE(a.start_date::text, ''),\n   481→         '',\n   482→         0,\n   483→         m.match_confidence::text,\n   484→         m.evidence\n   485→    FROM reviewed_vendor_entity_match m\n   486→    JOIN federal_award a ON a.id = m.source_pk\n   487→   WHERE m.organization_id = $1\n   488→     AND m.source_kind = 'federal_award_recipient'\n   489→)\n   490→SELECT DISTINCT ON (source_kind, source_name, detail)\n   491→       context_type, source_kind, source_label, COALESCE(source_name, ''),\n   492→       COALESCE(detail, ''), amount, COALESCE(record_year, 0), record_date,\n   493→       url, match_confidence, evidence\n   494→  FROM ctx\n   495→ ORDER BY source_kind, source_name, detail,\n   496→          context_type, record_year DESC NULLS LAST\n   497→ LIMIT 100;`\n   498→\trows, err := s.Pool.Query(ctx, q, organizationID)\n   499→\tif err != nil {\n   500→\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"organization public contexts: %w\", err)\n   501→\t}\n   502→\tdefer rows.Close()\n   503→\tout := []OrganizationPublicContext{}\n   504→\tfor rows.Next() {\n   505→\t\tvar c OrganizationPublicContext\n   506→\t\tvar evidence []byte\n   507→\t\tif err := rows.Scan(&c.ContextType, &c.SourceKind, &c.SourceLabel, &c.SourceName,\n   508→\t\t\t&c.Detail, &c.Amount, &c.RecordYear, &c.RecordDate, &c.URL,\n   509→\t\t\t&c.MatchConfidence, &evidence); err != nil {\n   510→\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"scan organization public context: %w\", err)\n   511→\t\t}\n   512→\t\tif len(evidence) > 0 {\n   513→\t\t\t_ = json.Unmarshal(evidence, &c.Evidence)\n   514→\t\t}\n   515→\t\tout = append(out, c)\n   516→\t}\n   517→\treturn out, rows.Err()\n   518→}\n   519→\n   520→type PopulateOrganizationsStats struct {\n   521→\tCandidatesProcessed   int\n   522→\tOrganizationsUpserted int\n   523→\tTestifiersLinked      int64\n   524→\tSkipped               int\n   525→\tVerified              int // matched against IRS BMF or PDC employer\n   526→}\n   527→\n   528→type PopulateOrganizationsProgress struct {\n   529→\tPhase       string\n   530→\tStats       PopulateOrganizationsStats\n   531→\tSourceName  string\n   532→\tElapsedTime time.Duration\n   533→}\n   534→\n   535→// PopulateOrganizationsFromCSIWithProgress seeds organization rows from\n   536→// distinct testifier.raw_organization values and links all matching testifier\n   537→// rows to the resulting organization. It is intentionally source-local: no\n   538→// cross-source merge is asserted here.\n   539→func (s *Store) PopulateOrganizationsFromCSIWithProgress(ctx context.Context, progress func(PopulateOrganizationsProgress)) (PopulateOrganizationsStats, error) {\n   540→\treturn s.populateOrganizationsFromCSI(ctx, 0, progress)\n   541→}\n   542→\n   543→func (s *Store) PopulateOrganizationsFromCSIForHearing(ctx context.Context, hearingID int64) (PopulateOrganizationsStats, error) {\n   544→\tif hearingID == 0 {\n   545→\t\treturn PopulateOrganizationsStats{}, errors.New(\"populate organizations for hearing: hearing id required\")\n   546→\t}\n   547→\treturn s.populateOrganizationsFromCSI(ctx, hearingID, nil)\n   548→}\n   549→\n   550→func (s *Store) populateOrganizationsFromCSI(ctx context.Context, hearingID int64, progress func(PopulateOrganizationsProgress)) (PopulateOrganizationsStats, error) {\n   551→\tconst q = `\n   552→WITH orgs AS (\n   553→    SELECT trim(t.raw_organization) AS source_name,\n   554→           wa_dd_normalize_entity_name(trim(t.raw_organization)) AS normalized_name,\n   555→           COUNT(*) AS occurrence_count\n   556→      FROM testifier t\n   557→      JOIN agenda_item a ON a.id = t.agenda_item_id\n   558→     WHERE NULLIF(trim(t.raw_organization), '') IS NOT NULL\n   559→       AND ($1::bigint = 0 OR a.hearing_id = $1)\n   560→     GROUP BY trim(t.raw_organization), wa_dd_normalize_entity_name(trim(t.raw_organization))\n   561→)\n   562→SELECT source_name, normalized_name\n   563→  FROM orgs\n   564→ WHERE normalized_name IS NOT NULL\n   565→ ORDER BY occurrence_count DESC, source_name;`\n   566→\tstarted := time.Now()\n   567→\temitProgress := func(phase string, stats PopulateOrganizationsStats, sourceName string) {\n   568→\t\tif progress == nil {\n   569→\t\t\treturn\n   570→\t\t}\n   571→\t\tprogress(PopulateOrganizationsProgress{\n   572→\t\t\tPhase:       phase,\n   573→\t\t\tStats:       stats,\n   574→\t\t\tSourceName:  sourceName,\n   575→\t\t\tElapsedTime: time.Since(started).Round(time.Second),\n   576→\t\t})\n   577→\t}\n   578→\n   579→\temitProgress(\"listing\", PopulateOrganizationsStats{}, \"\")\n   580→\trows, err := s.Pool.Query(ctx, q, hearingID)\n   581→\tif err != nil {\n   582→\t\treturn PopulateOrganizationsStats{}, fmt.Errorf(\"list CSI organizations: %w\", err)\n   583→\t}\n   584→\tdefer rows.Close()\n   585→\n   586→\tvar stats PopulateOrganizationsStats\n   587→\temitProgress(\"processing\", stats, \"\")\n   588→\tlastProgress := time.Now()\n   589→\tfor rows.Next() {\n   590→\t\tvar sourceName string\n   591→\t\tvar normalized *string\n   592→\t\tif err := rows.Scan(&sourceName, &normalized); err != nil      int\n   685→\tSkippedKeptID int // confidence != possible, left in place\n   686→}\n   687→\n   688→// PruneJunkOrganizations re-applies the junkOrganizationName heuristic to\n   689→// existing (anything other than 'possible') are\n   692→// preserved regardless. Returns counts for logging.\n   693→func (s *Store) PruneJunkOrganizations(ctx context.Context, dryRun bool) (PruneJunkOrganizationsStats, error) {\n   694→\tconst q = `\n   695→SELECT id, canonical_name, aliases, match_confidence::text\n   696→  FROM organization;`\n   697→\trows, err := s.Pool.Query(ctx, q)\n   698→\tif err != nil {\n   699→\t\treturn PruneJunkOrganizationsStats{}, fmt.Errorf(\"list organizations for prune: %w\", err)\n   700→\t}\n   701→\tdefer rows.Close()\n   702→\n   703→\ttype candidate struct {\n   704→\t\tid            int64\n   705→\t\tcanonicalName string\n   706→\t}\n   707→\tvar stats PruneJunkOrganizationsStats\n   708→\tvar toDelete []candidate\n   709→\tfor rows.Next() {\n   710→\t\tvar id int64\n   711→\t\tvar canonical, confidence string\n   712→\t\tvar aliases []string\n   713→\t\tif err := rows.Scan(&id, &canonical, &aliases, &confidence); err != nil {\n   714→\t\t\treturn stats, fmt.Errorf(\"scan organization: %w\", err)\n   715→\t\t}\n   716→\t\tstats.Scanned++\n   717→\t\tif confidence != \"possible\" {\n   718→\t\t\tstats.SkippedKeptID++\n   719→\t\t\tcontinue\n   720→\t\t}\n   721→\t\t// All names (canonical + aliases) must look like junk for the row to be\n   722→\t\t// pruned. Source-local: returning true means we will skip\n   820→// seeding an organization row, but the underlying testifier row is preserved.\n   821→func junkOrganizationName(raw, normalized string) bool {\n   822→\tr := strings.TrimSpace(raw)\n   823→\tn := strings.TrimSpace(normalized)\n   824→\tif r == \"\" || n == \"\" {\n   825→\t\treturn true\n   826→\t}\n   827→\tif len([]rune(n)) < 3 {\n   828→\t\treturn true\n   829→\t}\n   830→\tlow := strings.ToLower(r)\n   831→\n   832→\t// Exact matches: short self-descriptors and form sentinels.\n   833→\tif junkExactOrgNames[low] {\n   834→\t\treturn true\n   835→\t}\n   836→\n   837→\t// Wrapped in parens or brackets ((Retired), [Self], \"Home\", etc.) — almost\n   838→\t// always self-descriptors entered into the org column rather than a name.\n   839→\tif junkWrappedOrgRE.MatchString(r) {\n   840→\t\treturn true\n   841→\t}\n   842→\n   843→\t// Starts with '#': hashtag/handle noise (e.g. \"#NotABot\", \"#169\").\n   844→\tif strings.HasPrefix(r, \"#\") {\n   845→\t\treturn true\n   846→\t}\n   847→\n   848→\t// CSI form placeholder text leaking through verbatim.\n   849→\tif junkPlaceholderRE.MatchString(low) {\n   850→\t\treturn true\n   851→\t}\n   852→\n   853→\t// At least 50% of the runes must be letters;
